> AGENT

Found it. The problem is a **corrupted index file** in this worktree. Here's what's going on:

1. **The worktree index references a missing object** (`e53647ba...`) in its cache-tree — this is what `git status` tries to read and fails on.
2. **There are also 19+ missing blobs** — these were likely objects that existed temporarily (perhaps from shadow branch operations or a GC in the main repo) and got cleaned up.
3. The branch ref itself (`febabe6a`) is fine — your commits are intact.

The fix is to rebuild the worktree's index. The simplest way:

```
git read-tree HEAD
```

This re-reads the branch's tree into the index, clearing the corrupted cache-tree. However, **this will unstage any staged changes**. You currently have staged files (`.allowed-licenses`, `.claude-plugin/*`, etc.).

Do you want me to:

1. **Reset the index** with `git read-tree HEAD` — loses staged changes but fixes the issue
2. **Just clear the cache-tree** with `git update-index --force-untracked-cache` or by deleting/rebuilding the index more surgically
3. **Investigate more** — check whether the staged files are important first

Which approach do you prefer? Given the number of missing blobs, option 1 (full index rebuild) is probably the cleanest fix.

> AGENT

Fixed — clean status, no errors.

On the recurring issue: the missing blobs + corrupted cache-tree in the **worktree** index is a known pattern when `git gc` or `git prune` runs in the main repo and aggressively cleans up objects that the worktree index still references. This can happen when:

1. **`git gc --auto`** runs in the main repo (or another worktree) and prunes loose objects that this worktree's index cache-tree points to
2. **Shadow branch operations** (the `entire/` branches) create temporary trees/blobs that get committed to the shadow branch, but the worktree index caches references to the loose objects before they get packed — then GC prunes the loose copies

The fact that this worktree is inside `.worktrees/1` (a non-standard location vs `git worktree add`) might also contribute — git's GC doesn't always account for all worktree indexes when deciding what's reachable.

A few things that could help prevent it:
- Run `git config gc.worktreeRefExpire never` in the main repo to prevent GC from expiring worktree refs
- Check if something is triggering `git gc` automatically (e.g., a git hook, IDE, or the `gc.auto` setting)

Want me to look into whether your gc config or any hooks might be triggering this?

Found it. Here's the full picture:

**3 new pack files were created today at 11:08** — that's the signature of a `git gc` repack (3926 + 636 + 607 = ~5170 objects repacked into 3 packs).

```
git version 2.50.1 (Apple Git-155)
```

> AGENT

Here's the root cause chain:

1. **go-git's `SetEncodedObject` creates loose objects** — every checkpoint save creates blobs (session transcripts, metadata), trees, and commits as loose objects in `.git/objects/`. You currently have 4553 loose objects (and had more before GC ran).

2. **Loose object count exceeds `gc.auto` threshold** — the default is 6700. With frequent checkpoint saves, you hit this threshold.

3. **VS Code or Sourcetree triggers `gc --auto`** — both are running. VS Code's git extension periodically runs `git fetch`, and every git operation internally calls `gc --auto`. Your fetch reflog shows fetches happening multiple times daily. When `gc --auto` sees >6700 loose objects, it triggers a full GC.

4. **GC prunes loose objects the worktree index depends on** — this is the bug. GC repacks reachable objects from refs and prunes unreferenced loose objects. But the **worktree's index cache-tree and staged blobs** may reference loose objects that aren't reachable from any ref (e.g., staged but uncommitted content). GC in the main repo doesn't fully account for the worktree's index, so those blobs get pruned. The worktree index now points to missing objects → `fatal: unable to read`.

**The fix** — set `gc.auto=0` to disable auto-GC entirely, and run it manually when you want. Or increase the threshold significantly:

```
git config gc.auto 0
```

Want me to set that? You'd just run `git gc` manually when you feel like it (or we could add it to a hook at a safe point).

> AGENT

The worktree is properly registered with git and `.worktrees/` is gitignored. The location doesn't matter — worktrees can live anywhere on disk. What matters is:

- They all share the same `.git/objects/` store
- GC runs against that shared store
- Each worktree has its own index in `.git/worktrees/<name>/index`

The problem is the same regardless of where the worktree directory lives. It's the **go-git loose object accumulation → auto-GC → worktree index corruption** chain. A worktree at `/tmp/my-worktree` would hit the same issue.

Want me to disable auto-GC (`git config gc.auto 0`) to stop this from recurring?

> DEVELOPER

is it problematic, data wise?

> AGENT

Disabling auto-GC won't lose any data, but it will cause **disk bloat**:

- **Right now:** 502MB loose objects + 331MB in 29 pack files = ~833MB in `.git/objects/`
- go-git creates new loose objects on every checkpoint save (blobs for transcripts, trees, commits)
- Fetches keep creating new pack files without consolidation
- Over time git operations slow down as it searches more packs

So it's a tradeoff: corruption vs growing `.git` size.

A better fix would be to run GC only when it's safe — i.e., when worktree indexes are clean. You could:

1. **Run `git gc` manually after commits** (when indexes are freshly synced with HEAD, no staged content to lose)
2. Or add it to the `post-commit` hook — that's the safest moment since the index just matched a committed tree, so all referenced objects are reachable from refs

Honestly though, with 4 worktrees and frequent checkpoint operations, the cleanest fix is probably to **make the Entire CLI run `git gc` itself at a safe point** (e.g., after condensation when refs are updated) rather than letting it fire randomly from a VS Code fetch. Want me to look into that?

> DEVELOPER

```json
{
  "title": "Run git gc at safe points to prevent worktree index corruption",
  "team": "Entire",
  "project": "Troy",
  "description": "## Problem\n\ngo-git's `SetEncodedObject` creates loose objects in `.git/objects/` on every checkpoint save (blobs for session transcripts, trees, commits). When the loose object count exceeds the `gc.auto` threshold (default 6700), any git operation (e.g., VS Code/Sourcetree periodic fetch) triggers `git gc --auto`.\n\nGC repacks reachable objects and prunes loose objects, but doesn't fully account for worktree index cache-trees and staged blobs. This causes worktree index corruption:\n\n```\nfatal: unable to read <hash>\nerror: invalid sha1 pointer in cache-tree of .git/worktrees/1/index\nmissing blob <hash>\n```\n\nThis has happened twice. The fix is `git read-tree HEAD` which rebuilds the index but loses staged changes.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n1. Checkpoint saves accumulate loose objects (currently 4553 loose = ~502MB)\n2. Auto-GC triggers from background fetch (VS Code, Sourcetree)\n3. GC prunes loose objects referenced by worktree indexes but not by any ref\n4. Worktree index cache-tree points to missing objects → corruption\n\n## Proposed Solution\n\nRun `git gc` at safe points controlled by the CLI rather than letting it fire randomly:\n\n- After condensation (when refs are updated and indexes are clean)\n- After commit (post-commit hook, when index matches committed tree)\n- Or explicitly after checkpoint saves to ensure new objects are reachable from refs before GC can prune them\n\nThis avoids both the corruption (GC runs when safe) and disk bloat (GC still runs, just at controlled times).",
  "labels": [
    "improvement"
  ],
  "priority": 3
}
```

     1→# Known Limitations
     2→
     3→This document describes known limitations of the Entire CLI.
     4→
     5→## Git Operations
     6→
     7→### Amending Commits Loses Checkpoint Link
     8→
     9→When you amend a commit that has an `Entire-Checkpoint` trailer using `git commit --amend -m "new message"`, the checkpoint link is lost because the `-m` flag replaces the entire commit message.
    10→
    11→**Impact:**
    12→- The link between your code commit and the session metadata on `entire/sessions` is broken
    13→- `entire explain` can no longer find the associated session transcript
    14→- The checkpoint data still exists but is orphaned
    15→
    16→**Workarounds:**
    17→
    18→1. **Amend without `-m`**: Use `git commit --amend` (without `-m`) to open your editor, which preserves the existing message including the trailer
    19→
    20→2. **Manually preserve the trailer**: If you must use `-m`, first note the checkpoint ID:
    21→   ```bash
    22→   git log -1 --format=%B | grep "Entire-Checkpoint"
    23→   ```
    24→   Then include it in your new message:
    25→   ```bash
    26→   git commit --amend -m "new message
    27→
    28→   Entire-Checkpoint: <id-from-above>"
    29→   ```
    30→
    31→3. **Re-add after amend**: If you forgot, you can amend again to add the trailer back (if you still have the checkpoint ID from `entire explain` or the reflog)
    32→
    33→**Tracked in:** [ENT-161](https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-161)
    34→

But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.
</system-reminder>

This manifests as:\n\n```\nfatal: unable to read <hash>\nerror: invalid sha1 pointer in cache-tree of .git/worktrees/<n>/index\n```\n\n**Root cause:** Checkpoint saves use go-git's `SetEncodedObject` which creates loose objects. GC doesn't fully account for worktree index references when pruning, so objects get deleted while the worktree index still points to them.\n\n**Impact:**\n- `git status` fails in the affected worktree\n- Staged changes in the worktree are lost\n\n**Recovery:**\n```bash\n# In the affected worktree:\ngit read-tree HEAD\n```\nThis rebuilds the index from HEAD. Any previously staged changes will need to be re-staged.\n\n**Prevention:** Disable auto-GC and run it manually after commits (when indexes are clean):\n```bash\ngit config gc.auto 0\n```\n\n**Tracked in:** [ENT-241](https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-241)"
```json
}
```

> AGENT

So the answer to your question: **the migration is likely the trigger.** Here's why:

1. The migration on Feb 5 created **465 commits** on `entire/checkpoints/v1`, each with tree objects and blobs — that's thousands of loose objects in one shot
2. That pushed the loose count well past the 6700 `gc.auto` threshold
3. Since then, GC has been playing catch-up — each time it runs (triggered by fetch), it repacks some objects into packs (hence 12 + 7 + 3 new packs over 3 days), but there are still **4717 loose objects / 509MB** and **1057 prune-packable** objects sitting around
4. Each subsequent GC run is what's corrupting the worktree index

So the migration was indeed one-off, but it left behind a debris field of loose objects that keeps triggering GC. A single manual `git gc` from the main worktree (when this worktree's index is clean — which it is right now) would consolidate everything and stop the repeated auto-GC triggers. Want me to run it?

> DEVELOPER

and those phantom refs, I guess were from the old sessions branch?

> AGENT

Most likely, yeah. The migration cherry-picked 465 commits from `entire/sessions` to `entire/checkpoints/v1`. The old `entire/sessions` branch was presumably deleted after migration, but the blobs from its tree were still loose objects at that point. When GC ran, it pruned them (correctly — they were unreachable), but fsck was still reporting them as "missing" because some intermediate tree object in a pack still had dangling references to them.

Now that GC has done a full repack, those intermediate trees are gone too — clean slate.

> DEVELOPER

...sufficiently advanced technology...

> AGENT

...indistinguishable from magic. Or in this case, indistinguishable from cursed.

> DEVELOPER

learning Q: shouldn't the blobs have been reused in the new branch?

> AGENT

Good question. In git's content-addressable model, yes — same content = same SHA = same blob. If the migration preserved file contents byte-for-byte, the new branch's trees would point to the exact same blob objects and they'd remain reachable.

The missing blobs were likely from content that **changed** during migration. If the migration:

- Restructured the directory layout (old flat format → new sharded `<id[:2]>/<id[2:]>/` format)
- Re-serialized metadata.json (added fields, changed formatting, updated schema)
- Filtered out certain files or sessions

...then the new blobs would have different SHAs. The old blobs would only be reachable from the old `entire/sessions` branch. Once that branch was deleted, those blobs became unreachable and fair game for GC.

The **tree objects** would definitely differ (different directory structure = different tree hashes), but leaf blobs like `full.jsonl` or `prompt.txt` would survive if their content was byte-identical. The 103 missing blobs were probably the ones whose content was rewritten.

> DEVELOPER

AHHHHHHHHH yes that would be it. and it wouldn't have kept it as diffs?

> AGENT

Right — git stores **full snapshots**, not diffs. Every blob is the complete file content. Diffs are only computed on the fly (for `git diff`, `git log -p`, etc.).

The one place git *does* use deltas is inside **pack files** — it delta-compresses similar objects to save space. But that's a storage optimization, not a logical reference. The delta base and the delta target are both independent objects. If the base gets pruned, the delta is meaningless.

So when the old `entire/sessions` branch was deleted:
- The old blobs (full file contents) became unreachable
- Even if a pack file had delta-compressed a new blob against an old one, GC would resolve that during repacking — the new blob gets re-delta'd against something that *is* reachable
- The old blobs get pruned

No way to keep them alive without a ref pointing to them (or an ancestor of them).

> DEVELOPER

I did a git read-tree HEAD to resolve

> AGENT

So it happened right after the GC. That narrows it down — something is actively corrupting the worktree index, not just old debris.

### Critical: PostCommit Handler

**File:** `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_hooks.go`
**Lines 401-550: `PostCommit()` function**

This function DOES NOT directly modify the worktree index. However, it calls:
- `CondenseSession()` (line 479) which is defined in `manual_commit_condensation.go`

### In Condensation Logic

**File:** `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/manual_commit_condensation.go`
**Line 159-180: `store.WriteCommitted()` call**

The `WriteCommitted()` call does NOT modify the worktree index. It operates exclusively on the git object database (refs, trees, commits) on the `entire/sessions` orphan branch.

### In Checkpoint Package

**File:** `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/cmd/entire/cli/checkpoint/committed.go`
**Lines 40-100: `WriteCommitted()` function**

This function:
- Opens the repository with `git.PlainOpenWithOptions()` (line 837 in another part)
- Reads entries from the `entire/sessions` branch
- Builds git trees using `BuildTreeFromEntries()`
- Creates commits using `createCommit()`
- Updates the `entire/sessions` branch reference

**None of these operations touch the working tree index.**

## Answer to Your Question

**No recent gc detected.** The repository shows:
- Index file exists and is clean (working tree clean)
- Object database shows 255 object directories (00-ff) indicating normal state
- `packed-refs` file is 8.3KB and last modified Feb 12 12:04
- No `.git/objects/pack/` directory with gc-related files
- Most recent reflog shows pull/rebase operations, not gc

## Key Finding

The **index-modifying operations** (`worktree.Add()`) found in `/Users/alex/workspace/cli/cmd/entire/cli/strategy/common.go` (lines 967, 984) are part of staging logic, but they're only called in **test code and specific helper functions**, NOT in the PostCommit handler or condensation path.

The `PostCommit()` handler path:
1. PostCommit() → calls CondenseSession()
2. CondenseSession() → calls store.WriteCommitted()
3. WriteCommitted() → writes to `entire/sessions` branch (object database, not worktree index)

**The PostCommit handler does NOT modify the working tree index.**

```
   472→
   473→// OpenRepository opens the git repository with linked worktree support enabled.
   474→// It uses git.PlainOpenWithOptions with EnableDotGitCommonDir set to true,
   475→// which is required for proper operation in git worktrees created via 'git worktree add'.
   476→//
   477→// Without EnableDotGitCommonDir, go-git operations in worktrees can silently fail:
   478→// - Commits appear to succeed but are not persisted
   479→// - Refs are written to incorrect locations
   480→// - The worktree's HEAD/index don't get updated properly
   481→//
   482→// This happens because worktrees use .git as a file (pointing to the main repo)
   483→// rather than a directory, and go-git needs to route paths correctly between
   484→// shared (.git/) and per-worktree (.git/worktrees/<name>/) locations.
   485→//
   486→// The function first uses 'git rev-parse --show-toplevel' to find the repository
   487→// root, which works correctly even when called from a subdirectory within the repo.
   488→func OpenRepository() (*git.Repository, error) {
```

```
    23→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) SaveChanges(ctx SaveContext) error {
    24→	repo, err := OpenRepository()
    25→	if err != nil {
    26→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to open git repository: %w", err)
    27→	}
    28→
    29→	// Extract session ID from metadata dir
    30→	sessionID := filepath.Base(ctx.MetadataDir)
    31→
    32→	// Load or initialize session state
    33→	state, err := s.loadSessionState(sessionID)
    34→	if err != nil {
    35→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to load session state: %w", err)
    36→	}
    37→	// Initialize if state is nil OR BaseCommit is empty (can happen with partial state from warnings)
    38→	if state == nil || state.BaseCommit == "" {
    39→		// Preserve existing AgentType if we have a partial state, otherwise use default
    40→		agentType := DefaultAgentType
    41→		if state != nil && state.AgentType != "" {
    42→			agentType = state.AgentType
    43→		}
    44→		state, err = s.initializeSession(repo, sessionID, agentType, "") // No transcript path in fallback
    45→		if err != nil {
    46→			return fmt.Errorf("failed to initialize session: %w", err)
    47→		}
    48→	}
    49→
    50→	// Check if HEAD has changed (e.g., Claude did a rebase via tool call) and migrate if needed
    51→	if err := s.migrateAndPersistIfNeeded(repo, state); err != nil {
    52→		return err
    53→	}
    54→
    55→	// Get checkpoint store
    56→	store, err := s.getCheckpointStore()
    57→	if err != nil {
    58→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to get checkpoint store: %w", err)
    59→	}
    60→
    61→	// Check if shadow branch exists to report whether we created it
    62→	shadowBranchName := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)
    63→	branchExisted := store.ShadowBranchExists(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)
    64→
    65→	// Use the pending attribution calculated at prompt start (in InitializeSession)
    66→	// This was calculated BEFORE the agent made changes, so it accurately captures user edits
    67→	var promptAttr PromptAttribution
    68→	if state.PendingPromptAttribution != nil {
    69→		promptAttr = *state.PendingPromptAttribution
    70→		state.PendingPromptAttribution = nil // Clear after use
    71→	} else {
    72→		// No pending attribution (e.g., first checkpoint or session initialized without it)
    73→		promptAttr = PromptAttribution{CheckpointNumber: state.CheckpointCount + 1}
    74→	}
    75→
    76→	// Log the prompt attribution for debugging
    77→	attrLogCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "attribution")
    78→	logging.Debug(attrLogCtx, "prompt attribution at checkpoint save",
    79→		slog.Int("checkpoint_number", promptAttr.CheckpointNumber),
    80→		slog.Int("user_added", promptAttr.UserLinesAdded),
    81→		slog.Int("user_removed", promptAttr.UserLinesRemoved),
    82→		slog.Int("agent_added", promptAttr.AgentLinesAdded),
    83→		slog.Int("agent_removed", promptAttr.AgentLinesRemoved),
    84→		slog.String("session_id", sessionID))
    85→
    86→	// Use WriteTemporary to create the checkpoint
    87→	isFirstCheckpointOfSession := state.CheckpointCount == 0
    88→	result, err := store.WriteTemporary(context.Background(), checkpoint.WriteTemporaryOptions{
    89→		SessionID:         sessionID,
    90→		BaseCommit:        state.BaseCommit,
    91→		WorktreeID:        state.WorktreeID,
    92→		ModifiedFiles:     ctx.ModifiedFiles,
    93→		NewFiles:          ctx.NewFiles,
    94→		DeletedFiles:      ctx.DeletedFiles,
    95→		MetadataDir:       ctx.MetadataDir,
    96→		MetadataDirAbs:    ctx.MetadataDirAbs,
    97→		CommitMessage:     ctx.CommitMessage,
    98→		AuthorName:        ctx.AuthorName,
    99→		AuthorEmail:       ctx.AuthorEmail,
   100→		IsFirstCheckpoint: isFirstCheckpointOfSession,
   101→	})
   102→	if err != nil {
   103→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to write temporary checkpoint: %w", err)
   104→	}
   105→
   106→	// If checkpoint was skipped due to deduplication (no changes), return early
   107→	if result.Skipped {
   108→		logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   109→		logging.Info(logCtx, "checkpoint skipped (no changes)",
   110→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   111→			slog.String("checkpoint_type", "session"),
   112→			slog.Int("checkpoint_count", state.CheckpointCount),
   113→			slog.String("shadow_branch", shadowBranchName),
   114→		)
   115→		fmt.Fprintf(os.Stderr, "Skipped checkpoint (no changes since last checkpoint)\n")
   116→		return nil
   117→	}
   118→
   119→	// Update session state
   120→	state.CheckpointCount++
   121→
   122→	// Store the prompt attribution we calculated before saving
   123→	state.PromptAttributions = append(state.PromptAttributions, promptAttr)
   124→
   125→	// Track touched files (modified, new, and deleted)
   126→	state.FilesTouched = mergeFilesTouched(state.FilesTouched, ctx.ModifiedFiles, ctx.NewFiles, ctx.DeletedFiles)
   127→
   128→	// On first checkpoint, store the initial transcript position
   129→	if isFirstCheckpointOfSession {
   130→		state.TranscriptLinesAtStart = ctx.TranscriptLinesAtStart
   131→		state.TranscriptIdentifierAtStart = ctx.TranscriptIdentifierAtStart
   132→	}
   133→
   134→	// Accumulate token usage
   135→	if ctx.TokenUsage != nil {
   136→		state.TokenUsage = accumulateTokenUsage(state.TokenUsage, ctx.TokenUsage)
   137→	}
   138→
   139→	// Save updated state
   140→	if err := s.saveSessionState(state); err != nil {
   141→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to save session state: %w", err)
   142→	}
```

```
    31→const (
    32→	// ShadowBranchPrefix is the prefix for shadow branches.
    33→	ShadowBranchPrefix = "entire/"
    34→
    35→	// ShadowBranchHashLength is the number of hex characters used in shadow branch names.
    36→	// Shadow branches are named "entire/<hash>" using the first 7 characters of the commit hash.
    37→	ShadowBranchHashLength = 7
    38→
    39→	// WorktreeIDHashLength is the number of hex characters used for worktree ID hash.
    40→	WorktreeIDHashLength = 6
    41→)
    42→
    43→// HashWorktreeID returns a short hash of the worktree identifier.
    44→// Used to create unique shadow branch names per worktree.
    45→func HashWorktreeID(worktreeID string) string {
    46→	h := sha256.Sum256([]byte(worktreeID))
    47→	return hex.EncodeToString(h[:])[:WorktreeIDHashLength]
    48→}
    49→
    50→// WriteTemporary writes a temporary checkpoint to a shadow branch.
    51→// Shadow branches are named entire/<base-commit-short-hash>.
    52→// Returns the result containing commit hash and whether it was skipped.
    53→// If the new tree hash matches the last checkpoint's tree hash, the checkpoint
    54→// is skipped to avoid duplicate commits (deduplication).
    55→func (s *GitStore) WriteTemporary(ctx context.Context, opts WriteTemporaryOptions) (WriteTemporaryResult, error) {
    56→
    57→	// Validate base commit - required for shadow branch naming
    58→	if opts.BaseCommit == "" {
    59→		return WriteTemporaryResult{}, errors.New("BaseCommit is required for temporary checkpoint")
    60→	}
    61→
    62→	// Validate session ID to prevent path traversal
    63→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(opts.SessionID); err != nil {
    64→		return WriteTemporaryResult{}, fmt.Errorf("invalid temporary checkpoint options: %w", err)
    65→	}
    66→
    67→	// Get shadow branch name
    68→	shadowBranchName := ShadowBranchNameForCommit(opts.BaseCommit, opts.WorktreeID)
    69→
    70→	// Get or create shadow branch
    71→	parentHash, baseTreeHash, err := s.getOrCreateShadowBranch(shadowBranchName)
    72→	if err != nil {
    73→		return WriteTemporaryResult{}, fmt.Errorf("failed to get shadow branch: %w", err)
    74→	}
    75→
    76→	// Get the last checkpoint's tree hash for deduplication
    77→	var lastTreeHash plumbing.Hash
    78→	if parentHash != plumbing.ZeroHash {
    79→		if lastCommit, err := s.repo.CommitObject(parentHash); err == nil {
    80→			lastTreeHash = lastCommit.TreeHash
    81→		}
    82→	}
    83→
    84→	// Collect all files to include
    85→	var allFiles []string
    86→	var allDeletedFiles []string
    87→	if opts.IsFirstCheckpoint {
    88→		// For the first checkpoint, capture all changed files (modified tracked + untracked)
    89→		// using `git status --porcelain -z` which respects both repo and global .gitignore.
    90→		// This is much faster than filesystem walk. The base tree from HEAD already contains
    91→		// all unchanged tracked files. We also capture user's pre-existing deletions.
    92→		result, err := collectChangedFiles(ctx, s.repo)
    93→		if err != nil {
    94→			return WriteTemporaryResult{}, fmt.Errorf("failed to collect changed files: %w", err)
    95→		}
    96→		allFiles = result.Changed
    97→		// Merge user's pre-existing deletions with agent's deletions
    98→		allDeletedFiles = result.Deleted
    99→		allDeletedFiles = append(allDeletedFiles, opts.DeletedFiles...)
   100→	} else {
   101→		// For subsequent checkpoints, only include modified/new files
   102→		allFiles = make([]string, 0, len(opts.ModifiedFiles)+len(opts.NewFiles))
   103→		allFiles = append(allFiles, opts.ModifiedFiles...)
   104→		allFiles = append(allFiles, opts.NewFiles...)
   105→		allDeletedFiles = opts.DeletedFiles
   106→	}
   107→
   108→	// Build tree with changes
   109→	treeHash, err := s.buildTreeWithChanges(baseTreeHash, allFiles, allDeletedFiles, opts.MetadataDir, opts.MetadataDirAbs)
   110→	if err != nil {
   111→		return WriteTemporaryResult{}, fmt.Errorf("failed to build tree: %w", err)
   112→	}
   113→
   114→	// Deduplication: skip if tree hash matches the last checkpoint
   115→	if lastTreeHash != plumbing.ZeroHash && treeHash == lastTreeHash {
   116→		return WriteTemporaryResult{
   117→			CommitHash: parentHash,
   118→			Skipped:    true,
   119→		}, nil
   120→	}
   121→
   122→	// Create checkpoint commit with trailers
   123→	commitMsg := trailers.FormatShadowCommit(opts.CommitMessage, opts.MetadataDir, opts.SessionID)
   124→
   125→	commitHash, err := s.createCommit(treeHash, parentHash, commitMsg, opts.AuthorName, opts.AuthorEmail)
   126→	if err != nil {
   127→		return WriteTemporaryResult{}, fmt.Errorf("failed to create commit: %w", err)
   128→	}
   129→
   130→	// Update branch reference
   131→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   132→	newRef := plumbing.NewHashReference(refName, commitHash)
   133→	if err := s.repo.Storer.SetReference(newRef); err != nil {
   134→		return WriteTemporaryResult{}, fmt.Errorf("failed to update branch reference: %w", err)
   135→	}
   136→
   137→	return WriteTemporaryResult{
   138→		CommitHash: commitHash,
   139→		Skipped:    false,
   140→	}, nil
   141→}
   142→
   143→// ReadTemporary reads the latest checkpoint from a shadow branch.
   144→// Returns nil if the shadow branch doesn't exist.
   145→// worktreeID should be empty for main worktree or the internal git worktree name for linked worktrees.
   146→func (s *GitStore) ReadTemporary(ctx context.Context, baseCommit, worktreeID string) (*ReadTemporaryResult, error) {
   147→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   148→
   149→	shadowBranchName := ShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID)
   150→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   151→
   152→	ref, err := s.repo.Reference(refName, true)
   153→	if err != nil {
   154→		return nil, nil //nolint:nilnil,nilerr // Branch not found is an expected case
   155→	}
   156→
   157→	commit, err := s.repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   158→	if err != nil {
   159→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit object: %w", err)
   160→	}
   161→
   162→	// Extract session ID and metadata dir from commit trailers
   163→	sessionID, _ := trailers.ParseSession(commit.Message)
   164→	metadataDir, _ := trailers.ParseMetadata(commit.Message)
   165→
   166→	return &ReadTemporaryResult{
   167→		CommitHash:  ref.Hash(),
   168→		TreeHash:    commit.TreeHash,
   169→		SessionID:   sessionID,
   170→		MetadataDir: metadataDir,
   171→		Timestamp:   commit.Author.When,
   172→	}, nil
   173→}

   175→// ListTemporary lists all shadow branches with their checkpoint info.
   176→func (s *GitStore) ListTemporary(ctx context.Context) ([]TemporaryInfo, error) {
   177→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use

   179→	iter, err := s.repo.Branches()

   183→
   184→	var results []TemporaryInfo
   185→	err = iter.ForEach(func(ref *plumbing.Reference) error {
   186→		branchName := ref.Name().Short()
   187→		if !strings.HasPrefix(branchName, ShadowBranchPrefix) {
   188→			return nil
   189→		}
   190→
   191→		// Skip the sessions branch
   192→		if branchName == paths.MetadataBranchName {
   193→			return nil
   194→		}
   195→
   196→		commit, commitErr := s.repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   197→		if commitErr != nil {
   198→			//nolint:nilerr // Skip branches we can't read (non-fatal)
   199→			return nil
   200→		}
   201→
   202→		sessionID, _ := trailers.ParseSession(commit.Message)
   203→
   204→		// Extract base commit from branch name (handles new "entire/<commit>-<worktreeHash>" format)
   205→		baseCommit, _, _ := ParseShadowBranchName(branchName)
   206→
   207→		results = append(results, TemporaryInfo{
   208→			BranchName:   branchName,
   209→			BaseCommit:   baseCommit,
   210→			LatestCommit: ref.Hash(),
   211→			SessionID:    sessionID,
   212→			Timestamp:    commit.Author.When,
   213→		})
   214→
   215→		return nil
   216→	})
   217→
   218→	if err != nil {
   219→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to iterate branches: %w", err)
   220→	}
   221→
   222→	return results, nil
   223→}
   224→
   225→// WriteTemporaryTask writes a task checkpoint to a shadow branch.
   226→// Task checkpoints include both code changes and task-specific metadata.
   227→// Returns the commit hash of the created checkpoint.
   228→func (s *GitStore) WriteTemporaryTask(ctx context.Context, opts WriteTemporaryTaskOptions) (plumbing.Hash, error) {
   229→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   230→
   231→	// Validate base commit - required for shadow branch naming
   232→	if opts.BaseCommit == "" {
   233→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, errors.New("BaseCommit is required for task checkpoint")
   234→	}
   235→
   236→	// Validate identifiers to prevent path traversal and malformed data
   237→	if err := validation.ValidateSessionID(opts.SessionID); err != nil {
   238→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("invalid task checkpoint options: %w", err)
   239→	}
   240→	if err := validation.ValidateToolUseID(opts.ToolUseID); err != nil {
   241→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("invalid task checkpoint options: %w", err)
   242→	}
   243→	if err := validation.ValidateAgentID(opts.AgentID); err != nil {
   244→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("invalid task checkpoint options: %w", err)
   245→	}
   246→
   247→	// Get shadow branch name
   248→	shadowBranchName := ShadowBranchNameForCommit(opts.BaseCommit, opts.WorktreeID)
   249→
   250→	// Get or create shadow branch
   251→	parentHash, baseTreeHash, err := s.getOrCreateShadowBranch(shadowBranchName)
   252→	if err != nil {
   253→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to get shadow branch: %w", err)
   254→	}
   255→
   256→	// Collect all files to include in the commit
   257→	allFiles := make([]string, 0, len(opts.ModifiedFiles)+len(opts.NewFiles))
   258→	allFiles = append(allFiles, opts.ModifiedFiles...)
   259→	allFiles = append(allFiles, opts.NewFiles...)
   260→
   261→	// Build new tree with code changes (no metadata dir yet)
   262→	newTreeHash, err := s.buildTreeWithChanges(baseTreeHash, allFiles, opts.DeletedFiles, "", "")
   263→	if err != nil {
   264→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to build tree: %w", err)
   265→	}
   266→
   267→	// Add task metadata to tree
   268→	newTreeHash, err = s.addTaskMetadataToTree(newTreeHash, opts)
   269→	if err != nil {
   270→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to add task metadata: %w", err)
   271→	}
   272→
   273→	// Create the commit
   274→	commitHash, err := s.createCommit(newTreeHash, parentHash, opts.CommitMessage, opts.AuthorName, opts.AuthorEmail)
   275→	if err != nil {
   276→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to create commit: %w", err)
   277→	}
   278→
   279→	// Update shadow branch reference
   280→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   281→	ref := plumbing.NewHashReference(refName, commitHash)
   282→	if err := s.repo.Storer.SetReference(ref); err != nil {
   283→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to update shadow branch reference: %w", err)
   284→	}
   285→
   286→	return commitHash, nil
   287→}
   288→
   289→// addTaskMetadataToTree adds task checkpoint metadata to a git tree.
   290→// When IsIncremental is true, only adds the incremental checkpoint file.
   291→func (s *GitStore) addTaskMetadataToTree(baseTreeHash plumbing.Hash, opts WriteTemporaryTaskOptions) (plumbing.Hash, error) {
   292→	// Get base tree and flatten it
   293→	baseTree, err := s.repo.TreeObject(baseTreeHash)
   294→	if err != nil {
   295→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to get base tree: %w", err)
   296→	}
   297→
   298→	entries := make(map[string]object.TreeEntry)
   299→	if err := FlattenTree(s.repo, baseTree, "", entries); err != nil {
   300→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to flatten tree: %w", err)
   301→	}
   302→
   303→	// Compute metadata paths
   304→	sessionMetadataDir := paths.EntireMetadataDir + "/" + opts.SessionID
   305→	taskMetadataDir := sessionMetadataDir + "/tasks/" + opts.ToolUseID
   306→
   307→	if opts.IsIncremental {
   308→		// Incremental checkpoint: only add the checkpoint file
   309→		// Use proper JSON marshaling to handle nil/empty IncrementalData correctly
   310→		incrementalCheckpoint := struct {
   311→			Type      string          `json:"type"`
   312→			ToolUseID string          `json:"tool_use_id"`
   313→			Timestamp time.Time       `json:"timestamp"`
   314→			Data      json.RawMessage `json:"data"`
   315→		}{
   316→			Type:      opts.IncrementalType,
   317→			ToolUseID: opts.ToolUseID,
   318→			Timestamp: time.Now().UTC(),
   319→			Data:      opts.IncrementalData,
   320→		}
   321→		cpData, err := jsonutil.MarshalIndentWithNewline(incrementalCheckpoint, "", "  ")
   322→		if err != nil {
   323→			return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to marshal incremental checkpoint: %w", err)
   324→		}
   325→
   326→		cpBlobHash, err := CreateBlobFromContent(s.repo, cpData)
   327→		if err != nil {
   328→			return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to create incremental checkpoint blob: %w", err)
   329→		}
   330→		cpFilename := fmt.Sprintf("%03d-%s.json", opts.IncrementalSequence, opts.ToolUseID)
   331→		cpPath := taskMetadataDir + "/checkpoints/" + cpFilename
   332→		entries[cpPath] = object.TreeEntry{
   333→			Name: cpPath,
   334→			Mode: filemode.Regular,
   335→			Hash: cpBlobHash,
   336→		}
   337→	} else {
   338→		// Final checkpoint: add transcripts and checkpoint.json
   339→
   340→		// Add session transcript (with chunking support for large transcripts)
   341→		if opts.TranscriptPath != "" {
   342→			if transcriptContent, readErr := os.ReadFile(opts.TranscriptPath); readErr == nil {
   343→				// Detect agent type from content for proper chunking
   344→				agentType := agent.DetectAgentTypeFromContent(transcriptContent)
   345→
   346→				// Chunk if necessary
   347→				chunks, chunkErr := agent.ChunkTranscript(transcriptContent, agentType)
   348→				if chunkErr != nil {
   349→					logging.Warn(context.Background(), "failed to chunk transcript, checkpoint will be saved without transcript",
   350→						slog.String("error", chunkErr.Error()),
   351→						slog.String("session_id", opts.SessionID),
   352→					)
   353→				} else {
   354→					for i, chunk := range chunks {
   355→						chunkPath := sessionMetadataDir + "/" + agent.ChunkFileName(paths.TranscriptFileName, i)
   356→						blobHash, blobErr := CreateBlobFromContent(s.repo, chunk)
   357→						if blobErr != nil {
   358→							logging.Warn(context.Background(), "failed to create blob for transcript chunk",
   359→								slog.String("error", blobErr.Error()),
   360→								slog.String("session_id", opts.SessionID),
   361→								slog.Int("chunk_index", i),
   362→							)
   363→							continue
   364→						}
   365→						entries[chunkPath] = object.TreeEntry{
   366→							Name: chunkPath,
   367→							Mode: filemode.Regular,
   368→							Hash: blobHash,
   369→						}
   370→					}
   371→				}
   372→			}
   373→		}
   374→
   375→		// Add subagent transcript if available
   376→		if opts.SubagentTranscriptPath != "" && opts.AgentID != "" {
   377→			if agentContent, readErr := os.ReadFile(opts.SubagentTranscriptPath); readErr == nil {
   378→				if blobHash, blobErr := CreateBlobFromContent(s.repo, agentContent); blobErr == nil {
   379→					agentPath := taskMetadataDir + "/agent-" + opts.AgentID + ".jsonl"
   380→					entries[agentPath] = object.TreeEntry{
   381→						Name: agentPath,
   382→						Mode: filemode.Regular,
   383→						Hash: blobHash,
   384→					}
   385→				}
   386→			}
   387→		}
   388→
   389→		// Add checkpoint.json
   390→		checkpointJSON := fmt.Sprintf(`{
   391→  "session_id": %q,
   392→  "tool_use_id": %q,
   393→  "checkpoint_uuid": %q,
   394→  "agent_id": %q
   395→}`, opts.SessionID, opts.ToolUseID, opts.CheckpointUUID, opts.AgentID)
   396→
   397→		blobHash, err := CreateBlobFromContent(s.repo, []byte(checkpointJSON))
   398→		if err != nil {
   399→			return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to create checkpoint blob: %w", err)
   400→		}
   401→		checkpointPath := taskMetadataDir + "/checkpoint.json"
   402→		entries[checkpointPath] = object.TreeEntry{
   403→			Name: checkpointPath,
   404→			Mode: filemode.Regular,
   405→			Hash: blobHash,
   406→		}
   407→	}
   408→
   409→	// Build new tree from entries
   410→	return BuildTreeFromEntries(s.repo, entries)
   411→}
   412→
   413→// ListTemporaryCheckpoints lists all checkpoint commits on a shadow branch.
   414→// This returns individual commits (rewind points), not just branch info.
   415→// The sessionID filter, if provided, limits results to commits from that session.
   416→// worktreeID should be empty for main worktree or the internal git worktree name for linked worktrees.
   417→func (s *GitStore) ListTemporaryCheckpoints(ctx context.Context, baseCommit, worktreeID, sessionID string, limit int) ([]TemporaryCheckpointInfo, error) {
   418→	shadowBranchName := ShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID)
   419→	return s.listCheckpointsForBranch(ctx, shadowBranchName, sessionID, limit)
   420→}
   421→
   422→// ListCheckpointsForBranch lists checkpoint commits for a shadow branch by name.
   423→// Use this when you already have the full branch name (e.g., from ListTemporary).
   424→// The sessionID filter, if provided, limits results to commits from that session.
   425→func (s *GitStore) ListCheckpointsForBranch(ctx context.Context, branchName, sessionID string, limit int) ([]TemporaryCheckpointInfo, error) {
   426→	return s.listCheckpointsForBranch(ctx, branchName, sessionID, limit)
   427→}
   428→
   429→// listCheckpointsForBranch lists checkpoint commits for a specific shadow branch name.
   430→// This is an internal helper used by ListTemporaryCheckpoints, ListCheckpointsForBranch, and ListAllTemporaryCheckpoints.
   431→func (s *GitStore) listCheckpointsForBranch(ctx context.Context, shadowBranchName, sessionID string, limit int) ([]TemporaryCheckpointInfo, error) {
   432→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   433→
   434→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   435→
   436→	ref, err := s.repo.Reference(refName, true)
   437→	if err != nil {
   438→		return nil, nil //nolint:nilerr // No shadow branch is expected case
   439→	}
   440→
   441→	iter, err := s.repo.Log(&git.LogOptions{From: ref.Hash()})
   442→	if err != nil {
   443→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit log: %w", err)
   444→	}
   445→
   446→	var results []TemporaryCheckpointInfo
   447→	count := 0
   448→
   449→	err = iter.ForEach(func(c *object.Commit) error {
   450→		if count >= limit*5 { // Scan more to allow for session filtering
   451→			return errStop
   452→		}
   453→		count++
   454→
   455→		// Verify commit belongs to target session via Entire-Session trailer
   456→		commitSessionID, hasTrailer := trailers.ParseSession(c.Message)
   457→		if !hasTrailer {
   458→			return nil // Skip commits without session trailer
   459→		}
   460→		if sessionID != "" && commitSessionID != sessionID {
   461→			return nil // Skip commits from other sessions
   462→		}
   463→
   464→		// Get first line of message
   465→		message := c.Message
   466→		if idx := strings.Index(message, "\n"); idx > 0 {
   467→			message = message[:idx]
   468→		}
   469→
   470→		info := TemporaryCheckpointInfo{
   471→			CommitHash: c.Hash,
   472→			Message:    message,
   473→			SessionID:  commitSessionID,
   474→			Timestamp:  c.Author.When,
   475→		}
   476→
   477→		// Check for task checkpoint first
   478→		taskMetadataDir, foundTask := trailers.ParseTaskMetadata(c.Message)
   479→		if foundTask {
   480→			info.IsTaskCheckpoint = true
   481→			info.MetadataDir = taskMetadataDir
   482→			info.ToolUseID = extractToolUseIDFromPath(taskMetadataDir)
   483→		} else {
   484→			metadataDir, found := trailers.ParseMetadata(c.Message)
   485→			if found {
   486→				info.MetadataDir = metadataDir
   487→			}
   488→		}
   489→
   490→		results = append(results, info)
   491→
   492→		if len(results) >= limit {
   493→			return errStop
   494→		}
   495→		return nil
   496→	})
   497→
   498→	if err != nil && !errors.Is(err, errStop) {
   499→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("error iterating commits: %w", err)
   500→	}
   501→
   502→	return results, nil
   503→}
   504→
   505→// ListAllTemporaryCheckpoints lists checkpoint commits from ALL shadow branches.
   506→// This is used for checkpoint lookup when the base commit is unknown (e.g., HEAD advanced since session start).
   507→// The sessionID filter, if provided, limits results to commits from that session.
   508→func (s *GitStore) ListAllTemporaryCheckpoints(ctx context.Context, sessionID string, limit int) ([]TemporaryCheckpointInfo, error) {
   509→	_ = ctx // Reserved for future use
   510→
   511→	// List all shadow branches
   512→	branches, err := s.ListTemporary(ctx)
   513→	if err != nil {
   514→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to list shadow branches: %w", err)
   515→	}
   516→
   517→	var results []TemporaryCheckpointInfo
   518→
   519→	// Iterate through each shadow branch and collect checkpoints
   520→	for _, branch := range branches {
   521→		// Use the branch name directly to get checkpoints
   522→		branchCheckpoints, branchErr := s.listCheckpointsForBranch(ctx, branch.BranchName, sessionID, limit)
   523→		if branchErr != nil {
   524→			continue // Skip branches we can't read
   525→		}
   526→		results = append(results, branchCheckpoints...)
   527→		if len(results) >= limit {
   528→			results = results[:limit]
   529→			break
   530→		}
   531→	}
   532→
   533→	return results, nil
   534→}
   535→
   536→// extractToolUseIDFromPath extracts the ToolUseID from a task metadata directory path.
   537→// Task metadata dirs have format: .entire/metadata/<session>/tasks/<toolUseID>
   538→func extractToolUseIDFromPath(metadataDir string) string {
   539→	parts := strings.Split(metadataDir, "/")
   540→	if len(parts) >= 2 && parts[len(parts)-2] == "tasks" {
   541→		return parts[len(parts)-1]
   542→	}
   543→	return ""
   544→}
   545→
   546→// errStop is a sentinel error used to break out of git log iteration.
   547→var errStop = errors.New("stop iteration")
   548→
   549→// GetTranscriptFromCommit retrieves the transcript from a specific commit's tree.
   550→// This is used for shadow branch checkpoints where the transcript is stored in the commit tree
   551→// rather than on the entire/sessions branch.
   552→// commitHash is the commit to read from, metadataDir is the path within the tree.
   553→// agentType is used for reassembling chunked transcripts in the correct format.
   554→// Handles both chunked and non-chunked transcripts.
   555→func (s *GitStore) GetTranscriptFromCommit(commitHash plumbing.Hash, metadataDir string, agentType agent.AgentType) ([]byte, error) {
   556→	commit, err := s.repo.CommitObject(commitHash)
   557→	if err != nil {
   558→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit: %w", err)
   559→	}
   560→
   561→	tree, err := commit.Tree()
   562→	if err != nil {
   563→		return nil, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit tree: %w", err)
   564→	}
   565→
   566→	// Try to get the metadata subtree for chunk detection
   567→	subTree, subTreeErr := tree.Tree(metadataDir)
   568→	if subTreeErr == nil {
   569→		// Use the helper function that handles chunking
   570→		transcript, err := readTranscriptFromTree(subTree, agentType)
   571→		if err == nil && transcript != nil {
   572→			return transcript, nil
   573→		}
   574→	}
   575→
   576→	// Fall back to direct file access (for backwards compatibility)
   577→	transcriptPath := metadataDir + "/" + paths.TranscriptFileName
   578→	if file, fileErr := tree.File(transcriptPath); fileErr == nil {
   579→		content, contentErr := file.Contents()
   580→		if contentErr == nil {
   581→			return []byte(content), nil
   582→		}
   583→	}
   584→
   585→	transcriptPath = metadataDir + "/" + paths.TranscriptFileNameLegacy
   586→	if file, fileErr := tree.File(transcriptPath); fileErr == nil {
   587→		content, contentErr := file.Contents()
   588→		if contentErr == nil {
   589→			return []byte(content), nil
   590→		}
   591→	}
   592→
   593→	return nil, ErrNoTranscript
   594→}
   595→
   596→// ShadowBranchExists checks if a shadow branch exists for the given base commit and worktree.
   597→// worktreeID should be empty for main worktree or the internal git worktree name for linked worktrees.
   598→func (s *GitStore) ShadowBranchExists(baseCommit, worktreeID string) bool {
   599→	shadowBranchName := ShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID)
   600→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   601→	_, err := s.repo.Reference(refName, true)
   602→	return err == nil
   603→}
   604→
   605→// DeleteShadowBranch deletes the shadow branch for the given base commit and worktree.
   606→// worktreeID should be empty for main worktree or the internal git worktree name for linked worktrees.
   607→func (s *GitStore) DeleteShadowBranch(baseCommit, worktreeID string) error {
   608→	shadowBranchName := ShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID)
   609→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   610→	if err := s.repo.Storer.RemoveReference(refName); err != nil {
   611→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to delete shadow branch %s: %w", shadowBranchName, err)
   612→	}
   613→	return nil
   614→}
   615→
   616→// ShadowBranchNameForCommit returns the shadow branch name for a base commit hash
   617→// and worktree identifier. The worktree ID should be empty for the main worktree
   618→// or the internal git worktree name for linked worktrees.
   619→// Format: entire/<commit[:7]>-<hash(worktreeID)[:6]>
   620→func ShadowBranchNameForCommit(baseCommit, worktreeID string) string {
   621→	commitPart := baseCommit
   622→	if len(baseCommit) >= ShadowBranchHashLength {
   623→		commitPart = baseCommit[:ShadowBranchHashLength]
   624→	}
   625→	worktreeHash := HashWorktreeID(worktreeID)
   626→	return ShadowBranchPrefix + commitPart + "-" + worktreeHash
   627→}
   628→
   629→// ParseShadowBranchName extracts the commit prefix and worktree hash from a shadow branch name.
   630→// Input format: "entire/<commit[:7]>-<worktreeHash[:6]>"
   631→// Returns (commitPrefix, worktreeHash, ok). Returns ("", "", false) if not a valid shadow branch.
   632→func ParseShadowBranchName(branchName string) (commitPrefix, worktreeHash string, ok bool) {
   633→	if !strings.HasPrefix(branchName, ShadowBranchPrefix) {
   634→		return "", "", false
   635→	}
   636→	suffix := strings.TrimPrefix(branchName, ShadowBranchPrefix)
   637→
   638→	// Find the last dash - everything before is commit prefix, after is worktree hash
   639→	lastDash := strings.LastIndex(suffix, "-")
   640→	if lastDash == -1 || lastDash == 0 || lastDash == len(suffix)-1 {
   641→		// No dash, or dash at start/end - invalid format
   642→		// Could be old format "entire/<commit[:7]>" without worktree hash
   643→		return suffix, "", true // Return as commit prefix with empty worktree hash
   644→	}
   645→
   646→	return suffix[:lastDash], suffix[lastDash+1:], true
   647→}
   648→
   649→// getOrCreateShadowBranch gets or creates the shadow branch for checkpoints.
   650→// Returns (parentHash, baseTreeHash, error).
   651→func (s *GitStore) getOrCreateShadowBranch(branchName string) (plumbing.Hash, plumbing.Hash, error) {
   652→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(branchName)
   653→	ref, err := s.repo.Reference(refName, true)
   654→
   655→	if err == nil {
   656→		// Branch exists
   657→		commit, err := s.repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())
   658→		if err != nil {
   659→			return plumbing.ZeroHash, plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to get commit object: %w", err)
   660→		}
   661→		return ref.Hash(), commit.TreeHash, nil
   662→	}
   663→
   664→	// Branch doesn't exist, use current HEAD's tree as base
   665→	head, err := s.repo.Head()
   666→	if err != nil {
   667→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD: %w", err)
   668→	}
   669→
   670→	headCommit, err := s.repo.CommitObject(head.Hash())
   671→	if err != nil {
   672→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to get HEAD commit: %w", err)
   673→	}
   674→
   675→	return plumbing.ZeroHash, headCommit.TreeHash, nil
   676→}
   677→
   678→// buildTreeWithChanges builds a git tree with the given changes.
   679→// metadataDir is the relative path for git tree entries, metadataDirAbs is the absolute path
   680→// for filesystem operations (needed when CLI is run from a subdirectory).
   681→func (s *GitStore) buildTreeWithChanges(
   682→	baseTreeHash plumbing.Hash,
   683→	modifiedFiles, deletedFiles []string,
   684→	metadataDir, metadataDirAbs string,
   685→) (plumbing.Hash, error) {
   686→	// Get repo root for resolving file paths
   687→	// This is critical because fileExists() and createBlobFromFile() use os.Stat()
   688→	// which resolves relative to CWD. The modifiedFiles are repo-relative paths,
   689→	// so we must resolve them against repo root, not CWD.
   690→	repoRoot, err := paths.RepoRoot()
   691→	if err != nil {
   692→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to get repo root: %w", err)
   693→	}
   694→
   695→	// Get the base tree
   696→	baseTree, err := s.repo.TreeObject(baseTreeHash)
   697→	if err != nil {
   698→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to get base tree: %w", err)
   699→	}
   700→
   701→	// Flatten existing tree
   702→	entries := make(map[string]object.TreeEntry)
   703→	if err := FlattenTree(s.repo, baseTree, "", entries); err != nil {
   704→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to flatten base tree: %w", err)
   705→	}
   706→
   707→	// Remove deleted files
   708→	for _, file := range deletedFiles {
   709→		delete(entries, file)
   710→	}
   711→
   712→	// Add/update modified files
   713→	for _, file := range modifiedFiles {
   714→		// Resolve path relative to repo root for filesystem operations
   715→		absPath := filepath.Join(repoRoot, file)
   716→		if !fileExists(absPath) {
   717→			delete(entries, file)
   718→			continue
   719→		}
   720→
   721→		blobHash, mode, err := createBlobFromFile(s.repo, absPath)
   722→		if err != nil {
   723→			// Skip files that can't be staged (may have been deleted since detection)
   724→			continue
   725→		}
   726→
   727→		entries[file] = object.TreeEntry{
   728→			Name: file,
   729→			Mode: mode,
   730→			Hash: blobHash,
   731→		}
   732→	}
   733→
   734→	// Add metadata directory files
   735→	if metadataDir != "" && metadataDirAbs != "" {
   736→		if err := addDirectoryToEntriesWithAbsPath(s.repo, metadataDirAbs, metadataDir, entries); err != nil {
   737→			return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to add metadata directory: %w", err)
   738→		}
   739→	}
   740→
   741→	// Build tree
   742→	return BuildTreeFromEntries(s.repo, entries)
   743→}
   744→
   745→// createCommit creates a commit object.
   746→func (s *GitStore) createCommit(treeHash, parentHash plumbing.Hash, message, authorName, authorEmail string) (plumbing.Hash, error) {
   747→	now := time.Now()
   748→	sig := object.Signature{
   749→		Name:  authorName,
   750→		Email: authorEmail,
   751→		When:  now,
   752→	}
   753→
   754→	commit := &object.Commit{
   755→		TreeHash:  treeHash,
   756→		Author:    sig,
   757→		Committer: sig,
   758→		Message:   message,
   759→	}
   760→
   761→	// Add parent if not a new branch
   762→	if parentHash != plumbing.ZeroHash {
   763→		commit.ParentHashes = []plumbing.Hash{parentHash}
   764→	}
   765→
   766→	obj := s.repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   767→	if err := commit.Encode(obj); err != nil {
   768→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to encode commit: %w", err)
   769→	}
   770→
   771→	hash, err := s.repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(obj)
   772→	if err != nil {
   773→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to store commit: %w", err)
   774→	}
   775→
   776→	return hash, nil
   777→}
   778→
   779→// Helper functions extracted from strategy/common.go
   780→// These are exported for use by strategy package (push_common.go, session_test.go)
   781→
   782→// FlattenTree recursively flattens a tree into a map of full paths to entries.
   783→func FlattenTree(repo *git.Repository, tree *object.Tree, prefix string, entries map[string]object.TreeEntry) error {
   784→	for _, entry := range tree.Entries {
   785→		fullPath := entry.Name
   786→		if prefix != "" {
   787→			fullPath = prefix + "/" + entry.Name
   788→		}
   789→
   790→		if entry.Mode == filemode.Dir {
   791→			// Recurse into subtree
   792→			subtree, err := repo.TreeObject(entry.Hash)
   793→			if err != nil {
   794→				return fmt.Errorf("failed to get subtree %s: %w", fullPath, err)
   795→			}
   796→			if err := FlattenTree(repo, subtree, fullPath, entries); err != nil {
   797→				return err
   798→			}
   799→		} else {
   800→			entries[fullPath] = object.TreeEntry{
   801→				Name: fullPath,
   802→				Mode: entry.Mode,
   803→				Hash: entry.Hash,
   804→			}
   805→		}
   806→	}
   807→	return nil
   808→}
   809→
   810→// fileExists checks if a file exists at the given path.
   811→func fileExists(path string) bool {
   812→	_, err := os.Stat(path)
   813→	return err == nil
   814→}
   815→
   816→// createBlobFromFile creates a blob object from a file in the working directory.
   817→func createBlobFromFile(repo *git.Repository, filePath string) (plumbing.Hash, filemode.FileMode, error) {
   818→	info, err := os.Stat(filePath)
   819→	if err != nil {
   820→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to stat file: %w", err)
   821→	}
   822→
   823→	// Determine file mode
   824→	mode := filemode.Regular
   825→	if info.Mode()&0o111 != 0 {
   826→		mode = filemode.Executable
   827→	}
   828→	if info.Mode()&os.ModeSymlink != 0 {
   829→		mode = filemode.Symlink
   830→	}
   831→
   832→	// Read file contents
   833→	content, err := os.ReadFile(filePath) //nolint:gosec // filePath comes from walking the repository
   834→	if err != nil {
   835→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to read file: %w", err)
   836→	}
   837→
   838→	// Create blob object
   839→	obj := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   840→	obj.SetType(plumbing.BlobObject)
   841→	obj.SetSize(int64(len(content)))
   842→
   843→	writer, err := obj.Writer()
   844→	if err != nil {
   845→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to get object writer: %w", err)
   846→	}
   847→
   848→	_, err = writer.Write(content)
   849→	if err != nil {
   850→		_ = writer.Close()
   851→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to write blob content: %w", err)
   852→	}
   853→	if err := writer.Close(); err != nil {
   854→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to close blob writer: %w", err)
   855→	}
   856→
   857→	hash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(obj)
   858→	if err != nil {
   859→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, 0, fmt.Errorf("failed to store blob object: %w", err)
   860→	}
   861→
   862→	return hash, mode, nil
   863→}
   864→
   865→// addDirectoryToEntriesWithAbsPath recursively adds all files in a directory to the entries map.
   866→func addDirectoryToEntriesWithAbsPath(repo *git.Repository, dirPathAbs, dirPathRel string, entries map[string]object.TreeEntry) error {
   867→	err := filepath.Walk(dirPathAbs, func(path string, info os.FileInfo, err error) error {
   868→		if err != nil {
   869→			return err
   870→		}
   871→		if info.IsDir() {
   872→			return nil
   873→		}
   874→
   875→		// Calculate relative path within the directory, then join with dirPathRel for tree entry
   876→		relWithinDir, err := filepath.Rel(dirPathAbs, path)
   877→		if err != nil {
   878→			return fmt.Errorf("failed to get relative path for %s: %w", path, err)
   879→		}
   880→
   881→		blobHash, mode, err := createBlobFromFile(repo, path)
   882→		if err != nil {
   883→			return fmt.Errorf("failed to create blob for %s: %w", path, err)
   884→		}
   885→
   886→		treePath := filepath.Join(dirPathRel, relWithinDir)
   887→		entries[treePath] = object.TreeEntry{
   888→			Name: treePath,
   889→			Mode: mode,
   890→			Hash: blobHash,
   891→		}
   892→		return nil
   893→	})
   894→	if err != nil {
   895→		return fmt.Errorf("failed to walk directory %s: %w", dirPathAbs, err)
   896→	}
   897→	return nil
   898→}
   899→
   900→// treeNode represents a node in our tree structure.
   901→type treeNode struct {
   902→	entries map[string]*treeNode // subdirectories
   903→	files   []object.TreeEntry   // files in this directory
   904→}
   905→
   906→// BuildTreeFromEntries builds a proper git tree structure from flattened file entries.
   907→// Exported for use by strategy package (push_common.go, session_test.go)
   908→func BuildTreeFromEntries(repo *git.Repository, entries map[string]object.TreeEntry) (plumbing.Hash, error) {
   909→	// Build a tree structure
   910→	root := &treeNode{
   911→		entries: make(map[string]*treeNode),
   912→		files:   []object.TreeEntry{},
   913→	}
   914→
   915→	// Insert all entries into the tree structure
   916→	for fullPath, entry := range entries {
   917→		parts := strings.Split(fullPath, "/")
   918→		insertIntoTree(root, parts, entry)
   919→	}
   920→
   921→	// Recursively build tree objects from bottom up
   922→	return buildTreeObject(repo, root)
   923→}
   924→
   925→// insertIntoTree inserts a file entry into the tree structure.
   926→func insertIntoTree(node *treeNode, pathParts []string, entry object.TreeEntry) {
   927→	if len(pathParts) == 1 {
   928→		// This is a file in the current directory
   929→		node.files = append(node.files, object.TreeEntry{
   930→			Name: pathParts[0],
   931→			Mode: entry.Mode,
   932→			Hash: entry.Hash,
   933→		})
   934→		return
   935→	}
   936→
   937→	// This is in a subdirectory
   938→	dirName := pathParts[0]
   939→	if node.entries[dirName] == nil {
   940→		node.entries[dirName] = &treeNode{
   941→			entries: make(map[string]*treeNode),
   942→			files:   []object.TreeEntry{},
   943→		}
   944→	}
   945→	insertIntoTree(node.entries[dirName], pathParts[1:], entry)
   946→}
   947→
   948→// buildTreeObject recursively builds tree objects from a treeNode.
   949→func buildTreeObject(repo *git.Repository, node *treeNode) (plumbing.Hash, error) {
   950→	var treeEntries []object.TreeEntry
   951→
   952→	// Add files
   953→	treeEntries = append(treeEntries, node.files...)
   954→
   955→	// Recursively build subtrees
   956→	for name, subnode := range node.entries {
   957→		subHash, err := buildTreeObject(repo, subnode)
   958→		if err != nil {
   959→			return plumbing.ZeroHash, err
   960→		}
   961→		treeEntries = append(treeEntries, object.TreeEntry{
   962→			Name: name,
   963→			Mode: filemode.Dir,
   964→			Hash: subHash,
   965→		})
   966→	}
   967→
   968→	// Sort entries (git requires sorted entries)
   969→	sortTreeEntries(treeEntries)
   970→
   971→	// Create tree object
   972→	tree := &object.Tree{Entries: treeEntries}
   973→
   974→	obj := repo.Storer.NewEncodedObject()
   975→	if err := tree.Encode(obj); err != nil {
   976→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to encode tree: %w", err)
   977→	}
   978→
   979→	hash, err := repo.Storer.SetEncodedObject(obj)
   980→	if err != nil {
   981→		return plumbing.ZeroHash, fmt.Errorf("failed to store tree: %w", err)
   982→	}
   983→
   984→	return hash, nil
   985→}
   986→
   987→// sortTreeEntries sorts tree entries in git's required order.
   988→// Git sorts tree entries by name, with directories having a trailing /
   989→func sortTreeEntries(entries []object.TreeEntry) {
   990→	sort.Slice(entries, func(i, j int) bool {
   991→		nameI := entries[i].Name
   992→		nameJ := entries[j].Name
   993→		if entries[i].Mode == filemode.Dir {
   994→			nameI += "/"
   995→		}
   996→		if entries[j].Mode == filemode.Dir {
   997→			nameJ += "/"
   998→		}
   999→		return nameI < nameJ
  1000→	})
  1001→}
  1002→
  1003→// collectChangedFiles collects all changed files (modified tracked + untracked non-ignored)
  1004→// using git CLI. This is much faster than filesystem walk and respects all gitignore sources
  1005→// including global gitignore (core.excludesfile).
  1006→//
  1007→// Uses git CLI instead of go-git because go-git's worktree.Status() does not respect
  1008→// global gitignore, which can cause globally ignored files to appear as untracked.
  1009→// See: https://github.com/entireio/cli/pull/129
  1010→//
  1011→// changedFilesResult contains both changed and deleted files from git status.
  1012→type changedFilesResult struct {
  1013→	Changed []string // Files to include (modified, added, untracked, renamed, etc.)
  1014→	Deleted []string // Files that were deleted (need to be excluded from checkpoint tree)
  1015→}
  1016→
  1017→// collectChangedFiles returns all changed files from git status for the first checkpoint.
  1018→//
  1019→// For the first checkpoint, we need to capture:
  1020→// - Modified tracked files (user's uncommitted changes)
  1021→// - Untracked non-ignored files (new files not yet added to git)
  1022→// - Renamed/copied files (both source removal and destination)
  1023→// - Deleted files (to exclude from checkpoint tree)
  1024→//
  1025→// The base tree from HEAD already contains all unchanged tracked files.
  1026→//
  1027→// Uses `git status --porcelain -z` for reliable parsing of filenames with special characters.
  1028→func collectChangedFiles(ctx context.Context, repo *git.Repository) (changedFilesResult, error) {
  1029→	// Get repo root directory for running git command
  1030→	wt, err := repo.Worktree()
  1031→	if err != nil {
  1032→		return changedFilesResult{}, fmt.Errorf("failed to get worktree: %w", err)
  1033→	}
  1034→	repoRoot := wt.Filesystem.Root()
  1035→
  1036→	// Use -z for NUL-separated output (handles quoted filenames with spaces/special chars)
  1037→	// Use -uall to list individual untracked files instead of collapsed directories.
  1038→	// Note: CLAUDE.md warns against -uall for user-facing display, but we need the full list
  1039→	// for checkpointing.
  1040→	cmd := exec.CommandContext(ctx, "git", "status", "--porcelain", "-z", "-uall")
  1041→	cmd.Dir = repoRoot
  1042→	output, err := cmd.Output()
  1043→	if err != nil {
  1044→		return changedFilesResult{}, fmt.Errorf("failed to get git status in %s: %w", repoRoot, err)
  1045→	}
  1046→
  1047→	changedSeen := make(map[string]struct{})
  1048→	deletedSeen := make(map[string]struct{})
  1049→
  1050→	// Parse NUL-separated output
  1051→	// Format: XY filename\0 (for most entries)
  1052→	// For renames/copies: XY newname\0oldname\0
  1053→	entries := strings.Split(string(output), "\x00")
  1054→
  1055→	for i := 0; i < len(entries); i++ {
  1056→		entry := entries[i]
  1057→		if len(entry) < 3 {
  1058→			continue
  1059→		}
  1060→
  1061→		// git status --porcelain format: XY filename
  1062→		// X = staging status, Y = worktree status
  1063→		staging := entry[0]
  1064→		wtStatus := entry[1]
  1065→		filename := entry[3:] // No TrimSpace needed with -z format
  1066→
  1067→		// Handle R/C (rename/copy) first - they have a second entry we must skip
  1068→		// even if the new filename is an infrastructure path
  1069→		if staging == 'R' || staging == 'C' {
  1070→			// Renamed or copied: current entry is new name, next entry is old name
  1071→			if !paths.IsInfrastructurePath(filename) {
  1072→				changedSeen[filename] = struct{}{}
  1073→			}
  1074→			// The old name follows as the next NUL-separated entry - must always skip it
  1075→			if i+1 < len(entries) && entries[i+1] != "" {
  1076→				oldName := entries[i+1]
  1077→				if staging == 'R' && !paths.IsInfrastructurePath(oldName) {
  1078→					// For renames, old file is effectively deleted
  1079→					deletedSeen[oldName] = struct{}{}
  1080→				}
  1081→				i++ // Skip the old name entry
  1082→			}
  1083→			continue
  1084→		}
  1085→
  1086→		// Skip .entire directory for non-R/C entries
  1087→		if paths.IsInfrastructurePath(filename) {
  1088→			continue
  1089→		}
  1090→
  1091→		// Handle different status codes
  1092→		switch {
  1093→		case staging == 'D' || wtStatus == 'D':
  1094→			// Deleted file - track separately
  1095→			deletedSeen[filename] = struct{}{}
  1096→
  1097→		case wtStatus == 'M' || wtStatus == 'A':
  1098→			// Modified or added in worktree
  1099→			changedSeen[filename] = struct{}{}
  1100→
  1101→		case staging == '?' && wtStatus == '?':
  1102→			// Untracked file
  1103→			changedSeen[filename] = struct{}{}
  1104→
  1105→		case staging == 'A' || staging == 'M':
  1106→			// Staged add or modify
  1107→			changedSeen[filename] = struct{}{}
  1108→
  1109→		case staging == 'T' || wtStatus == 'T':
  1110→			// Type change (e.g., file to symlink)
  1111→			changedSeen[filename] = struct{}{}
  1112→
  1113→		case staging == 'U' || wtStatus == 'U':
  1114→			// Unmerged (conflict) - include current file state
  1115→			changedSeen[filename] = struct{}{}
  1116→		}
  1117→	}
  1118→
  1119→	changed := make([]string, 0, len(changedSeen))
  1120→	for file := range changedSeen {
  1121→		changed = append(changed, file)
  1122→	}
  1123→
  1124→	deleted := make([]string, 0, len(deletedSeen))
  1125→	for file := range deletedSeen {
  1126→		deleted = append(deleted, file)
  1127→	}
  1128→
  1129→	return changedFilesResult{Changed: changed, Deleted: deleted}, nil
  1130→}
  1131→
```

> AGENT

Here's my analysis:

The stop hook code (SaveChanges → WriteTemporary) doesn't touch the worktree index at all — it only creates loose objects and updates branch refs. So the corruption isn't coming from go-git writing to the index.

The most likely cause: **go-git v5's `EnableDotGitCommonDir` has a bug with worktree index handling.** When `OpenRepository()` opens the repo from the worktree, go-git needs to split operations between shared storage (`.git/objects/`, `.git/refs/`) and per-worktree storage (`.git/worktrees/1/HEAD`, `.git/worktrees/1/index`). If `SetReference` or any ref update inadvertently triggers an index write or cache-tree rebuild through the worktree storage layer, it could corrupt the index.

This would explain:
- Why it only happens in the worktree, not the main repo
- Why it happens after hook operations that use go-git
- Why it started after you added `EnableDotGitCommonDir` (which was required for worktree support)

Two things worth checking:
1. Does the corruption happen if you run the same hooks from the **main** worktree?
2. Can you tell me exactly what the error was this time — was it the same `unable to read <hash>` / `invalid sha1 pointer in cache-tree`?

If it's the same pattern, I think we should add an `fsnotify` watch on the index file (or just `stat` before/after) in the hook code to confirm go-git is the one modifying it.

> AGENT

You're absolutely right. The stop hook is just the first thing that tried to use the index and discovered it was already broken. The actual corruption happened during `git add` / `git commit` / `git push`.

Let me check what actually touches the index during that sequence:

1. **`git add`** — writes index
2. **`git commit`** — reads index, builds trees, updates cache-tree, runs `gc --auto`
3. **prepare-commit-msg hook** — Entire CLI opens repo with go-git
4. **post-commit hook** — Entire CLI condensation via go-git
5. **`git push`** — reads refs
6. **pre-push hook** — Entire CLI pushes sessions branch via go-git

The prepare-commit-msg hook runs *during* the commit — between git reading the index and finalizing. Let me check what it does with go-git.

```
    27→// askConfirmTTY prompts the user for a yes/no confirmation via /dev/tty.
    28→// This works even when stdin is redirected (e.g., git commit -m).
    29→// Returns true for yes, false for no. If TTY is unavailable, returns the default.
    30→// If context is non-empty, it is displayed on a separate line before the prompt.
    31→func askConfirmTTY(prompt string, context string, defaultYes bool) bool {
    32→	// Open /dev/tty for both reading and writing
    33→	// This is the controlling terminal, which works even when stdin/stderr are redirected
    34→	tty, err := os.OpenFile("/dev/tty", os.O_RDWR, 0)
    35→	if err != nil {
    36→		// Can't open TTY (e.g., running in CI), use default
    37→		return defaultYes
    38→	}
    39→	defer tty.Close()
    40→
    41→	// Show context if provided
    42→	if context != "" {
    43→		fmt.Fprintf(tty, "%s\n", context)
    44→	}
    45→
    46→	// Show prompt with default indicator
    47→	// Write to tty directly, not stderr, since git hooks may redirect stderr to /dev/null
    48→	var hint string
    49→	if defaultYes {
    50→		hint = "[Y/n]"
    51→	} else {
    52→		hint = "[y/N]"
    53→	}
    54→	fmt.Fprintf(tty, "%s %s ", prompt, hint)
    55→
    56→	// Read response
    57→	reader := bufio.NewReader(tty)
    58→	response, err := reader.ReadString('\n')
    59→	if err != nil {
    60→		return defaultYes
    61→	}
    62→
    63→	response = strings.TrimSpace(strings.ToLower(response))
    64→	switch response {
    65→	case "y", "yes":
    66→		return true
    67→	case "n", "no":
    68→		return false
    69→	default:
    70→		// Empty or invalid input - use default
    71→		return defaultYes
    72→	}
    73→}
    74→
    75→// CommitMsg is called by the git commit-msg hook after the user edits the message.
    76→// If the message contains only our trailer (no actual user content), strip it
    77→// so git will abort the commit due to empty message.
    78→//
    79→//nolint:unparam // error return required by interface but hooks must return nil
    80→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) CommitMsg(commitMsgFile string) error {

    84→	}

    88→	// Check if our trailer is present (ParseCheckpoint validates format, so found==true means valid)
    89→	if _, found := trailers.ParseCheckpoint(message); !found {
    90→		// No trailer, nothing to do
    91→		return nil
    92→	}
    93→
    94→	// Check if there's any user content (non-comment, non-trailer lines)
    95→	if !hasUserContent(message) {
    96→		// No user content - strip the trailer so git aborts
    97→		message = stripCheckpointTrailer(message)
    98→		if err := os.WriteFile(commitMsgFile, []byte(message), 0o600); err != nil {
    99→			return nil //nolint:nilerr // Hook must be silent on failure
   100→		}
```

```
   175→// PrepareCommitMsg is called by the git prepare-commit-msg hook.
   176→// Adds an Entire-Checkpoint trailer to the commit message with a stable checkpoint ID.
   177→// Only adds a trailer if there's actually new session content to condense.
   178→// The actual condensation happens in PostCommit - if the user removes the trailer,
   179→// the commit will not be linked to the session (useful for "manual" commits).
   180→// For amended commits, preserves the existing checkpoint ID.
   181→//
   182→// The source parameter indicates how the commit was initiated:
   183→//   - "" or "template": normal editor flow - adds trailer with explanatory comment
   184→//   - "message": using -m or -F flag - prompts user interactively via /dev/tty
   185→//   - "merge", "squash", "commit": skip trailer entirely (auto-generated or amend commits)
   186→//
   187→
   188→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) PrepareCommitMsg(commitMsgFile string, source string) error {
   189→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "checkpoint")
   190→
   191→	// Skip during rebase, cherry-pick, or revert operations

   195→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   196→			slog.String("source", source),
   197→		)
   198→		return nil
   199→	}
   200→
   201→	// Skip for merge, squash, and commit (amend) sources
   202→	// These are auto-generated or reusing existing messages - not from Claude sessions
   203→	switch source {
   204→	case "merge", "squash", "commit":
   205→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prepare-commit-msg: skipped for source",
   206→			slog.String("strategy", "manual-commit"),
   207→			slog.String("source", source),
   208→		)
   209→		return nil
   210→	}
   211→	repo, err := OpenRepository()

   220→
   221→	// Find all active sessions for this worktree

   232→	}
   233→
   234→	// Check if any session has new content to condense

   237→	// Determine which checkpoint ID to use

   269→			}
```

```
   394→// PostCommit is called by the git post-commit hook after a commit is created.
   395→// Checks if the commit has an Entire-Checkpoint trailer and if so, condenses

   399→//
   400→//nolint:unparam // error return required by interface but hooks must return nil
   401→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) PostCommit() error {

   403→

   433→	}
```

> AGENT

**There it is.** Line 988: `worktree.Status()`.

`getStagedFiles` calls `repo.Worktree()` then `worktree.Status()`. In go-git v5, `worktree.Status()` **reads AND writes the index** — it loads the index, compares it against the working tree, and can update the index's cache-tree as a side effect.

This is called from **two places**:
- **Line 284** — `PrepareCommitMsg` (runs *during* `git commit`, while git itself is also reading the index)
- **Line 683** — in the post-commit detection logic

The `PrepareCommitMsg` call is the smoking gun. It runs **mid-commit** — git has already locked the index for the commit, and then go-git's `worktree.Status()` reads/writes the same index file concurrently. This is a race condition between git's commit process and go-git's index access.

Even if there's no lock conflict (git releases the lock before calling the hook), go-git's `worktree.Status()` may rebuild the cache-tree with stale data, and then git might overwrite it partially after the hook returns.

The fix: replace `getStagedFiles` with a `git diff --cached --name-only` CLI call instead of go-git's `worktree.Status()`. Want me to fix this?

> DEVELOPER

are you sure?

> AGENT

No, I'm not sure. I assumed `worktree.Status()` writes the index, but it's supposed to be read-only. Let me verify rather than speculate.

The simplest way to catch the actual culprit: add a stat check on the index file around each operation to see exactly when the modification time changes. Something like:

```bash
stat -f "%Sm" .git/worktrees/1/index
```

Before and after each step: `git add`, `git commit` (which runs prepare-commit-msg + post-commit hooks), `git push` (which runs pre-push hook), and the stop hook.

That would tell us exactly which operation corrupts the index. Want to try that next time it happens, or should I instrument the hook code to log the index mtime/checksum before and after each go-git operation?

> DEVELOPER

I've only recently started seeing this happen...but I also moved to 'persistent' worktrees in the last week
(previously had been blowing away worktrees and opening fresh ones)

> AGENT

That's the key clue. With fresh worktrees, any index corruption was invisible because you'd blow them away before seeing it. With persistent worktrees, the corruption survives and you hit it on the next operation.

And it rules out GC as the cause this second time — we had just cleaned the object store to zero loose objects. There's nothing for GC to prune.

So something in the commit/hook cycle is actively corrupting the worktree index. The `EnableDotGitCommonDir` suspicion is stronger now — go-git needs to route index reads/writes to `.git/worktrees/1/index` instead of `.git/index`. If it gets that wrong (reads one, writes the other, or mixes cache-tree data between them), you'd get exactly this symptom.

Let me instrument it. I'll add index file checksum logging before and after each go-git operation in the hooks, so next time it happens we know exactly which call corrupted it.

They're completely different files (different entry counts — `e7` vs fewer), but worktree 2 has the same md5 as what the post-commit hook produced in worktree 1. That's the smoking gun.

**The post-commit hook, running in worktree 1, is writing to worktree 2's index.** go-git with `EnableDotGitCommonDir` is resolving the index path to the wrong worktree.
