   595→	}
   596→
   597→	// Look for transcript file
   598→	metadataDir := paths.EntireMetadataDir + "/" + state.SessionID
   599→	var transcriptLines int
   600→
   601→	if file, fileErr := tree.File(metadataDir + "/" + paths.TranscriptFileName); fileErr == nil {
   602→		if content, contentErr := file.Contents(); contentErr == nil {
   603→			transcriptLines = countTranscriptLines(content)
   604→		}
   605→	} else if file, fileErr := tree.File(metadataDir + "/" + paths.TranscriptFileNameLegacy); fileErr == nil {
   606→		if content, contentErr := file.Contents(); contentErr == nil {
   607→			transcriptLines = countTranscriptLines(content)
   608→		}
   609→	}
   610→
   611→	// Has new content if there are more lines than already condensed
   612→	return transcriptLines > state.CondensedTranscriptLines, nil
   613→}
   614→
   615→// countTranscriptLines counts lines in a transcript, matching the counting method used
   616→// in extractSessionData for consistency. This trims trailing empty lines (from final \n
   617→// in JSONL) but includes empty lines in the middle of the file.
   618→func countTranscriptLines(content string) int {
   619→	lines := strings.Split(content, "\n")
   620→	// Trim trailing empty lines (from final \n in JSONL)
   621→	for len(lines) > 0 && strings.TrimSpace(lines[len(lines)-1]) == "" {
   622→		lines = lines[:len(lines)-1]
   623→	}
   624→	return len(lines)
   625→}
   626→
   627→// sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript checks if a session has new content
   628→// by examining the live transcript file. This is used when no shadow branch exists
   629→// (i.e., no Stop has happened yet) but the agent may have done work.
   630→//
   631→// Returns true if:
   632→//  1. The transcript has grown since the last condensation, AND
   633→//  2. The new transcript portion contains file modifications, AND
   634→//  3. At least one modified file overlaps with the currently staged files
   635→//
   636→// The overlap check ensures we don't add checkpoint trailers to commits that are
   637→// unrelated to the agent's recent changes.
   638→//
   639→// This handles the scenario where the agent commits mid-session before Stop.
   640→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript(repo *git.Repository, state *SessionState) (bool, error) {
   641→	// Need both transcript path and agent type to analyze
   642→	if state.TranscriptPath == "" || state.AgentType == "" {
   643→		return false, nil
   644→	}
   645→
   646→	// Get the agent for transcript analysis
   647→	ag, err := agent.GetByAgentType(state.AgentType)
   648→	if err != nil {
   649→		return false, nil //nolint:nilerr // Unknown agent type, fail gracefully
   650→	}
   651→
   652→	// Cast to TranscriptAnalyzer
   653→	analyzer, ok := ag.(agent.TranscriptAnalyzer)
   654→	if !ok {
   655→		return false, nil // Agent doesn't support transcript analysis
   656→	}
   657→
   658→	// Get current transcript position
   659→	currentPos, err := analyzer.GetTranscriptPosition(state.TranscriptPath)
   660→	if err != nil {
   661→		return false, nil //nolint:nilerr // Error reading transcript, fail gracefully
   662→	}
   663→
   664→	// Check if transcript has grown since last condensation
   665→	if currentPos <= state.CondensedTranscriptLines {
   666→		return false, nil // No new content
   667→	}
   668→
   669→	// Transcript has grown - check if there are file modifications in the new portion
   670→	modifiedFiles, _, err := analyzer.ExtractModifiedFilesFromOffset(state.TranscriptPath, state.CondensedTranscriptLines)
   671→	if err != nil {
   672→		return false, nil //nolint:nilerr // Error parsing transcript, fail gracefully
   673→	}
   674→
   675→	// No file modifications means no new content to checkpoint
   676→	if len(modifiedFiles) == 0 {
   677→		return false, nil

> AGENT

Good observation. Here's how I think both pieces fit together:

1. **Post-commit hook rewrites the index** — `worktree.Status()` reads the index and writes it back (go-git updates stat info as a cache optimization). This happens every time, but usually it's harmless.

2. **GC is the trigger for corruption** — when go-git writes the index back, it preserves (or recomputes) the cache-tree extension. If GC has just pruned tree objects that the cache-tree references, the rewritten index now points to missing objects.

So it's a **two-hit combo**:
- **Hit 1:** GC prunes some tree objects (loose objects that the cache-tree references but aren't reachable from any ref)
- **Hit 2:** go-git's `worktree.Status()` rewrites the index, preserving the stale cache-tree pointing to those pruned objects

Without GC, the objects still exist, so the stale cache-tree is harmless. Without `worktree.Status()` rewriting the index, git would lazily invalidate/rebuild the cache-tree itself. You need both to get the corruption.

The `e53647ba` hash being the same every time supports this — it's a specific tree object that go-git keeps putting in the cache-tree, and GC keeps pruning because nothing references it from a commit.

The fix is still the same: replace `getStagedFiles` with a git CLI call so go-git never touches the index. Want me to do that?

> AGENT

`getStagedFiles` has been there since the initial commit, but `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript` (which is the code path that calls `getStagedFiles` from PostCommit) was added recently — those commits are all from the ENT-221 branch work.

So before this change, `getStagedFiles` was only called from `PrepareCommitMsg` (line 284). The PostCommit path never called it. Now `PostCommit → filterSessionsWithNewContent → sessionHasNewContent → sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript → getStagedFiles → worktree.Status()` is a new code path.

**That's the change that introduced the bug.** The `sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript` function added a path where `worktree.Status()` gets called during post-commit, which rewrites the index. Combined with GC having pruned objects, the rewritten index has stale cache-tree entries.

The change you mentioned about replacing go-git with `git status` CLI was in `collectChangedFiles` — a different function. But the fix here is the same idea: replace `getStagedFiles`'s `worktree.Status()` with `git diff --cached --name-only`.

   877→func (s *ManualCommitStrategy) calculatePromptAttributionAtStart(
   878→	repo *git.Repository,
   879→	state *SessionState,
   880→) PromptAttribution {
   881→	logCtx := logging.WithComponent(context.Background(), "attribution")
   882→	nextCheckpointNum := state.CheckpointCount + 1
   883→	result := PromptAttribution{CheckpointNumber: nextCheckpointNum}
   884→
   885→	// Get last checkpoint tree from shadow branch (if it exists)
   886→	// For the first checkpoint, no shadow branch exists yet - this is fine,
   887→	// CalculatePromptAttribution will use baseTree as the reference instead.
   888→	var lastCheckpointTree *object.Tree
   889→	shadowBranchName := checkpoint.ShadowBranchNameForCommit(state.BaseCommit, state.WorktreeID)
   890→	refName := plumbing.NewBranchReferenceName(shadowBranchName)
   891→	ref, err := repo.Reference(refName, true)
   892→	if err != nil {
   893→		logging.Debug(logCtx, "prompt attribution: no shadow branch yet (first checkpoint)",
   894→			slog.String("shadow_branch", shadowBranchName))
   895→		// Continue with lastCheckpointTree = nil
   896→	} else {
   897→		shadowCommit, err := repo.CommitObject(ref.Hash())

   939→			slog.String("error", err.Error()))
   940→		return result
   941→	}

   945→	// Build map of changed files with their worktree content
   946→	// IMPORTANT: We read from worktree (not staging area) to match what WriteTemporary
   947→	// captures in checkpoints. This ensures attribution is consistent.
   948→	changedFiles := make(map[string]string)
   949→	for filePath, fileStatus := range status {

   952→			continue
   953→		}
   954→		// Skip .entire metadata directory (session data, not user code)

   963→			// Use git's binary detection algorithm (matches getFileContent behavior).
   964→			// Binary files are excluded from line-based attribution calculations.
   965→			isBinary, binErr := binary.IsBinary(bytes.NewReader(data))
   966→			if binErr == nil && !isBinary {
   967→				content = string(data)
   968→			}
   969→		}
   970→		// else: file deleted, unreadable, or binary - content remains empty string
   971→
   972→		changedFiles[filePath] = content
   973→	}

   976→	result = CalculatePromptAttribution(baseTree, lastCheckpointTree, changedFiles, nextCheckpointNum)

   978→	return result

{"id":"1879e1f3-c444-45fa-9e5b-ae644e57f599","identifier":"ENT-241","title":"Run git gc at safe points to prevent worktree index corruption","description":"## Problem\n\ngo-git's `SetEncodedObject` creates loose objects in `.git/objects/` on every checkpoint save (blobs for session transcripts, trees, commits). When the loose object count exceeds the `gc.auto` threshold (default 6700), any git operation (e.g., VS Code/Sourcetree periodic fetch) triggers `git gc --auto`.\n\nGC repacks reachable objects and prunes loose objects, but doesn't fully account for worktree index cache-trees and staged blobs. This causes worktree index corruption:\n\n```\nfatal: unable to read <hash>\nerror: invalid sha1 pointer in cache-tree of .git/worktrees/1/index\nmissing blob <hash>\n```\n\nThis has happened twice. The fix is `git read-tree HEAD` which rebuilds the index but loses staged changes.\n\n## Root Cause\n\n1. Checkpoint saves accumulate loose objects (currently 4553 loose = \\~502MB)\n2. Auto-GC triggers from background fetch (VS Code, Sourcetree)\n3. GC prunes loose objects referenced by worktree indexes but not by any ref\n4. Worktree index cache-tree points to missing objects → corruption\n\n## Proposed Solution\n\nRun `git gc` at safe points controlled by the CLI rather than letting it fire randomly:\n\n* After condensation (when refs are updated and indexes are clean)\n* After commit (post-commit hook, when index matches committed tree)\n* Or explicitly after checkpoint saves to ensure new objects are reachable from refs before GC can prune them\n\nThis avoids both the corruption (GC runs when safe) and disk bloat (GC still runs, just at controlled times).","priority":{"value":3,"name":"Medium"},"url":"https://linear.app/entirehq/issue/ENT-241/run-git-gc-at-safe-points-to-prevent-worktree-index-corruption","gitBranchName":"alex/ent-241-run-git-gc-at-safe-points-to-prevent-worktree-index","createdAt":"2026-02-07T00:27:51.475Z","updatedAt":"2026-02-07T00:27:51.475Z","archivedAt":null,"completedAt":null,"dueDate":null,"status":"Backlog","labels":["Improvement"],"attachments":[],"documents":[],"createdBy":"Alex Ong","createdById":"45a00cf6-3a37-45ab-a508-d9da7f9d514c","project":"Troy","projectId":"2eb22709-c7b4-4a11-9270-468079c0739f","team":"Entire","teamId":"832db0be-d9d8-45dc-9774-7f35babe62c0"}

```json
{
  "title": "Replace go-git worktree.Status() with git CLI to prevent index corruption",
  "team": "Entire",
  "project": "Troy",
  "priority": 1,
  "labels": [
    "Bug"
  ],
  "description": "## Problem\n\ngo-git's `worktree.Status()` reads **and rewrites** the worktree index file. When called from the `PostCommit` hook path, this rewrites the index with stale cache-tree entries that reference objects pruned by a previous GC. This causes worktree index corruption:\n\n```\nfatal: unable to read e53647ba1f1e5203bedff558b482f178db045555\n```\n\n### Root Cause\n\n**Two-hit combo:**\n1. **GC prunes objects** that the index cache-tree references (hit 1)\n2. **go-git's `worktree.Status()` rewrites the index**, preserving the stale cache-tree entries (hit 2)\n\nBoth are required for corruption. GC alone doesn't break things (native git commands read but don't rewrite the stale cache-tree). The go-git rewrite materializes the corruption.\n\n### Confirmed via instrumentation\n\nDebug hooks added to `prepare-commit-msg`, `post-commit`, and `pre-push` logged index md5 checksums before/after each hook. The post-commit hook was confirmed to change the index:\n\n```\n15:02:07 prepare-commit-msg BEFORE: 48f554e407de50ea53be22030173682f\n15:02:07 prepare-commit-msg AFTER:  48f554e407de50ea53be22030173682f  (unchanged)\n15:02:08 post-commit BEFORE: 48f554e407de50ea53be22030173682f\n15:02:27 post-commit AFTER:  bfb70aa91de94955ceda432ff3fcbaff  ← CHANGED\n```\n\n### Call chain\n\n```\nPostCommit()\n  → filterSessionsWithNewContent()\n    → sessionHasNewContent()\n      → sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript()\n        → getStagedFiles()\n          → worktree.Status()  ← rewrites index\n```\n\nIntroduced in commit `6d61daaa` (\"feat(manual-commit): detect mid-session commits via live transcript analysis\", Jan 22).\n\n### Why it's only visible now\n\nThe user recently moved to persistent worktrees. Previously worktrees were blown away and recreated, so stale cache-tree entries never persisted long enough to cause visible corruption.\n\n## Fix\n\nReplace `getStagedFiles()` in `manual_commit_hooks.go` (line 982) with `git diff --cached --name-only` CLI call. This reads the index without rewriting it.\n\n**Two call sites:**\n1. Line 284 (PrepareCommitMsg) - less critical but should still be fixed\n2. Line 683 (sessionHasNewContentFromLiveTranscript → PostCommit path) - **the confirmed culprit**\n\n### Additional occurrence\n\n`calculatePromptAttributionAtStart()` (line 936) also calls `worktree.Status()`. This is called at session start (not during git hooks) so it's lower risk, but should be evaluated for the same fix pattern.\n\n## Related\n\n- ENT-241: Run git gc at safe points (addresses hit 1 of the two-hit combo)\n- This issue addresses hit 2 (preventing go-git from rewriting the index)",
  "relatedTo": [
    "ENT-241"
  ]
}
```

```json
{
  "id": "ENT-241",
  "description": "## Problem\n\ngo-git's `SetEncodedObject` creates loose objects in `.git/objects/` on every checkpoint save (blobs for session transcripts, trees, commits). When the loose object count exceeds the `gc.auto` threshold (default 6700), any git operation (e.g., VS Code/Sourcetree periodic fetch) triggers `git gc --auto`.\n\nGC repacks reachable objects and prunes loose objects. Combined with go-git's `worktree.Status()` rewriting the index (see ENT-242), this causes worktree index corruption:\n\n```\nfatal: unable to read <hash>\nerror: invalid sha1 pointer in cache-tree of .git/worktrees/1/index\nmissing blob <hash>\n```\n\nThe fix is `git read-tree HEAD` which rebuilds the index but loses staged changes.\n\n## Root Cause (updated)\n\nThis is **hit 1 of a two-hit combo** (see ENT-242 for hit 2):\n\n1. **GC prunes objects** that the index cache-tree references ← this issue\n2. **go-git rewrites the index** preserving stale cache-tree entries ← ENT-242\n\nGC alone doesn't break things - native git commands can tolerate stale cache-tree entries (they just rebuild on next write). The corruption only manifests when go-git's `worktree.Status()` rewrites the index, materializing the stale references.\n\n## Object accumulation\n\n- Checkpoint saves create loose objects via `SetEncodedObject` (blobs, trees, commits)\n- A migration from `entire/sessions` to `entire/checkpoints/v1` created 465 checkpoint commits in 8 minutes\n- Loose objects accumulate until gc.auto threshold (6700) triggers auto-GC\n\n## Proposed Solution\n\nRun `git gc` at safe points controlled by the CLI rather than letting it fire randomly:\n\n* After condensation (when refs are updated and indexes are clean)\n* After commit (post-commit hook, when index matches committed tree)\n* Or explicitly after checkpoint saves to ensure new objects are reachable from refs before GC can prune them\n\nThis avoids both the corruption (GC runs when safe) and disk bloat (GC still runs, just at controlled times).\n\n## Priority Note\n\nENT-242 (replacing `worktree.Status()` with git CLI) is the higher priority fix since it prevents hit 2 entirely. This issue is a defense-in-depth measure to prevent hit 1."
}
```

> AGENT

Good point. If we fix ENT-242 (stop go-git from rewriting the index), then GC pruning objects is harmless on its own - native git commands tolerate stale cache-tree entries and silently rebuild them. Hit 1 without hit 2 doesn't cause corruption.

The only remaining concern from ENT-241 would be disk bloat from loose objects, but that's not a corruption issue.

I'd say ENT-241 can be closed (or at least deprioritized to nice-to-have). The real fix is ENT-242. Want me to close it?
